Votaer (VOH-tayr) is the port, because the crossing is why the people are there. It is one of the largest cities on this coast. It faces Orentel across the water. Tumair writes the hull: pilgrim, pot, person, or cargo. Lertho charges the Hush-rate on the same water. The White Note writes its terms on Orentel’s third quay. Towns and villages along the rest of the shore stay unnamed.

How it formed. Port-kings and charter-harbours grew from the crossing. The tongue stays conservative because the grove is at their backs, and theology stays mid because Lestrand is in front of them. A hinge has two sides. The Grey Summer taught the pious shore how to step around a fade without putting the blessing down.

Structure. It is a guild-coast, with a light state and old houses. Any Kind may take up the work. Far-Voiced on every quay as infrastructure, same as Strandoren, with more hymn left in the voice.

Goals. Keep the Old Crossing the world’s highway. Classify the hull before anyone prices it. Broker pilgrimage-traffic and cargo under the Shore’s own categories. Charge the Hush-rate. Say the blessing if a mouth wants one.

Its injustice. Category as fate. Lestrand will let you fade and feel it owed you nothing. The Hinge Shore will bless you as it files you, and a Tithe-poor household fails in sight of the pilgrimage-road with a hymn still in the air.

Lirorn: the Thaw-Land

(leer-ORN · lir “thaw; water that runs again” + orn “land” = “thaw-land.”) Theology mid · reach high · governance mid. Everyday Lirorn; people Lirorn-folk. The range divides Maiethorn’s waters. The name is the snowmelt.

Lirorn is Maiethorn’s central range. The snow is high, the towns are rope-and-wing, and the rivers begin as thaw. Reach is still full. Grafts take in the valleys and the sheltered shelves. The weather is bigger, and the state is a watershed holding. Lowlands drink Lirorn’s spring. Ridges notice.

Tengu ridge-towns and Fox-of-the-Snows hearths sit densest here. Mixed Kinds on every shelf. A lowlander arriving on foot is a guest who climbed.

Site. This range divides the continent’s watersheds. The older job of the ground is the ridge-roads. They used to be walking-paths to the grove. Stones sit in the high squares, and some roads now end at a local Tree. Tasain (tah-SAYN) holds the seat. The levy is taken where the west road comes down. It is a walled town in a sheltered valley below the Shelf-gate. The town gate is Tasain’s own. The Shelf-gate is the pass above it. The Noon Pass stays a pass, higher and older. Narol of the Pass is dead. The house still levies. Villages and hamlets stay on the shelves. Larbril is the city on the dry side of that pass. Tasain is where you are when the road has come down into shelter.

How it formed. It formed as march-holds on the snowmelt. Governance is mid because water is a fact you can levy without a census of souls: you tax the thaw, the pass, the high road. Theology is cooled by height and by distance from the grove’s daily pressure.

Structure. Ridge-compacts, pass-tolls, and valley charters sit under a holding. Any Kind may take up the work. Sky-couriers hold ordinary office.

Goals. Keep the passes and the spring. Be paid for water the core calls a gift. The people stay off the Threnhael. The Threnhael will name a pass when it can.

Its injustice. The ridge pays in isolation for water the valley drinks. The tolls feel like geography. A shelf-town whose graft sickens is a long way from a stiller. Mid-state enough to levy; not enough to furnish every Tithe on a cliff.

Brenledd: the Hearth-League

(bren-LED · bren “hearth” + ledd “to reckon” = “hearths of the ledger.”) Theology low · reach high · governance low. Everyday the Brenledd or the compact; people Brenled-folk. A league of hearths. The throne-city sits inside the league.

A league of charter-towns on Strandoren (river-ports, lesser harbours, inland markets) that compact to face Lestrand’s houses and stay many hearths. Each town is a hearth with its own Tree and its own vouching. The compact is the arithmetic: shared tickets, shared notes, shared refusal to be priced one at a time. The throne-city is Raitin (RAY-tin). The council sits there. It is a river-city, one of the largest on the Shore-lands. Six charter-towns sit around it. They stay unnamed. Nidtol is the hearth-name people still recite when Lestrand offers one throat. Raitin is the city where the notes clear. Turvo keeps the notes. Sulnu carries a cleared ticket to the river stair. Nubo is vouched in Raitin and still unknown in the next hearth. A throne-city leaves the league many hearths.

Faith worn light, same as the coast. Trees mature. Nothing is policed; everything is chartered.

Site. Brenledd sits behind and between the premier coast. The older job of the ground is salt and river towns. Some were road-end Tree-towns that federated instead of fading into a deathless house’s hinterland.

How it formed. Towns that got tired of being the other party on a Lestrand note. The compact is a pile of charters that learned to speak together, messy and still holding.

Structure. A council of hearths. You are of your town first. Any Kind may take up the work. A person vouched in one Brenled square may still be a stranger in the next. The compact moves coin better than it moves standing.

Goals. Keep many tickets cheaper than one house. Underwrite each other. Stay a league when Lestrand would rather negotiate with a single throat.

Its injustice. Falling between charters. Vouched here, unknown there; Tithe-provision a town matter the compact will not touch. Thin welfare in a rich network. The fade happens in the gaps the league calls local.

Leddvael: the Reckoned Gift

(led-VAYL · ledd “to reckon” + vael “gift” = “the gift reckoned.”) Motherfaith theology low; the Fair Hand worn as civic law. Reach high · governance mid. Everyday Leddvael; people Leddvael-folk. The name is the doctrine: a gift is a bargain priced. The Book-Hands stamp and recite; they have no seat. Bound Terms stay as written.

Leddvael is a signing-coast on Strandoren that took the Fair Hand from a dockside theology to a civic rite. Leaf-Fall is a festival and a signing-watch. Tithe is a term serviced. The Bound are model citizens here, employed as the shape of honesty. Mid governance: the book is a kind of statute, and persons stay off a census. They file a contract. The list stays Threnmaieth’s.

Site. This is a different stretch of indented coast than Lestrand, with enough water to have its own houses. The older job of the ground is a salt quay with a signing-watch. Naenor (NAY-nor) is the port, on a stretch of coast of its own. It is large, and slightly smaller than Orentel. Derdil stands the signing-watch. The Book-Hands stamp and recite in this wind. They hold no seat here. Bound Terms stay as written. The city wins the term and loses the berth. Empty prestige berths stay Orentel’s. Naenor has working berths. The work is the watch.

How it formed. Fortune-and-contract practice thickened until a charter-coast found it cheaper to establish the Settling than to argue hymns per dock. The Settling is Fair Hand contract-practice. A habit that got a seal.

Structure. A charter-council works with the book-tables. Any Kind may take up the work. Gift-reading families live here. They are the minority that sounds devout and looks like a credit risk.

Goals. Keep signings clear. Vouch the Struck briskly as a later contract. Treat Motherfaith gift-talk as unpriced language. It stays allowed.

Its injustice. Bargain-theology as statute. A Given child is a term opened. A household that insists the colour was a gift is a bad debtor in a signing-town. The Bound are safe and over-read: the world’s contract-pillar asked to be the world’s whole person. No villain-faith. A sincere ledger laid over real Conditions.

Trenledd: the Worn Count

(tren-LED · thren “sacred counting” + ledd “to reckon,” with the holy sound sanded off.) The reconstruction Threnledd is unused on purpose: they will not say the holy word. Theology low · reach high · governance high. Everyday Trenledd or the Trenled; people Trenled-folk. They call the list the roll, Lestrand’s word for a tax roll, here meaning persons. Dropping the hymn from the count is the thesis.

Trenledd is the wealthy interior of Strandoren. The Trees are mature, the Given are dense, the guilds are thick, and the state writes people down. The list began as ticket-and-tax machinery (safety-licences, house-notes, charter-rolls) and forgot to stop at property. There is no hymn that makes it stewardship. There is no Mother to be accounted to. You are on the roll because commerce and hazard-control wanted a name they could find again.

Threnmaieth at least claims to keep you hale. Trenledd claims to keep you findable. Cutting you off is default, not impiety.

Site. The interior is rich in Trees, and reach is high. The older job of the ground is road-end Tree-towns that got filed. Stones in the squares have numbers on the back. Lunbra (LUN-bra) is on the Chart-run, four to eight days upriver of Orentel. It is large and wealthy. The roll is kept here. The money is shown in the square. Vamar keeps a column. A neighbour says Vamar. A clerk says the house first: Melro, Vamar. The governing throat stays unnamed. Towns through the filed country stay unnamed.

How it formed. Guild-tickets plus deathless memory plus a council that distrusted living witness the way Threnmaieth does, without Threnmaieth’s scripture. The three pillars work here and are treated as insufficient. Feeling, word, and memory answer to themselves. A ledger answers to the desk. Each crisis added a column.

Structure. It is a writing-crown or a writing-council. Licence-guilds serve as state organs. Any Kind may take up the work. The Struck are pursued to be catalogued because an unwitnessed change is a row that appeared without a clerk.

Goals. Know who is dangerous, who owes, who Turned. Furnish Tithe through channels that can be shut. Argue procedure, and keep theology off the docket.

Its injustice. A census with the halo worn off. You cannot appeal to piety. You cannot shame them with “the Mother’s gifts should not be listed.” They agree, and list you anyway, as cargo and hazard. Colder than the Tallied Crown. This is the bureaucratic edge of the world’s small scare.

Netstrand: the Night Shore

(NET-strand · neth “night; the long dark” + strand “shore.”) Reconstruction Nethstrand unused in daily speech. Theology mid-low · reach high · governance low. Everyday Netstrand; people Netstrand-folk. People call it the Night Shore. West water, storm stories, and watches after dark are what a traveler meets there.

Netstrand is Strandoren’s west and south face. The open ocean lies toward Heskoren and, far beyond, the storm-wall. Bold houses run the frontier trade. They sell hulls and west-water berths — crews, capacity, a crossing quoted like a pilgrimage. The First-Hand year is assembled on the Old Crossing: Netstrand charters the ship, the White Note writes the terms, Orentel holds the berth. Orenbren houses the sleep at the origin. Almost none have crossed to the Sundering Isle. Those who claim to are the tellers of Yumboe stories.

Faith mixed: Motherfaith worn light, Fair Hand ordinary on a signing-quay, quay-charms nobody writes down. Trees still mature along this wealthy edge.

Site. This is an open-ocean coast. The older job of the ground is a salt quay. Eolthael berths sit empty. Holds smell of earth, and graft-pots go out. The neighbour’s week is billed as a crossing. Braetu (BRAY-too) is the harbour. It is smaller than Orentel, because the far run is thinner than the Old Crossing. Mursur writes the far crossing and takes the fee for a lamp on a name. They keep a dark book of quoted crossings that did not come back. One berth stays unlit, with no house attached. The city leaves the berth that way. The book on this quay is the Heskoren run. The Isle stays a tale. Towns along the rest of the shore stay unnamed.

How it formed. West-houses grew from Salt Walk traffic and the later carrying to Heskoren (by sea, coasts first, then the far). A shore that faces the wave’s supply-line.

Structure. Merchant houses and berth-guilds sit under a light charter-council. Any Kind may take up the work. Selkie are common on the water. Sky-couriers from Lirorn and Tengu families work the clock-towers. The towers are a job.

Goals. Quote the far crossing. Keep the Heskoren run profitable. Sell Isle-tales without having to survive them. Keep the berths on Netstrand’s own book.

Its injustice. The walk as inventory. Prestige priced; necessity billed; a neighbour’s week to a waiting cluster is a line on a docket.

Ornled: the Outer Ledger

(orn-LED · orn “land” + ledd “to reckon”; clipped from Ornledd.) On Heskoren, dropping the sacred sound is a neglect, not just distance. Theology low · reach low · governance low. Everyday Ornled; people Ornled-folk. Reconstruction Ornledd unused. They do not tend old endings. A Vaethorn without the faith, harder and colder.

Ornled is thin-reach Heskoren country that never took the waiting-gospel. It has the same young grafts, and the same Kept-heavy and Struck-heavy weather as Vaethorn, and no congregation to fill the gap. Tithe-provision is barter, favor, and a slate on the wall. An unvouched Struck is a problem met with a rope, a deal, or a neighbour. Custom outruns law because there is almost no law and almost no hymn.

They do not hate the Mother. They do not use her. Vaethorn hears the missing th in their name as godlessness. Ornled hears Vaethorn’s th as a luxury.

Site. These are frontier pockets off the hottest pilgrim-roads. The older job of the ground is a waiting cluster without a gospel. The stones are the same, and the upper rooms are the same if a walk used to pass. That older job is necessity. Sanbreo (san-BREE-oh) is where the slate is kept and a hull can pay a beach-fee. It is on the brink of a small city. The first line on the wall is still the sentence Vathne cut. Vathne is that line. No new person sits the gate. Villages in the pockets around it stay unnamed. Eolvaeth is Vaethorn’s seat. It is a pilgrim-edge vale in the waiting-gospel. Sanbreo keeps this gate.

How it formed. Kept and Struck-heavy districts watched the waiting-theology arrive with the grafts and declined to make scarcity a soul-problem. Improvisation is the culture. Governance is low because no one has the spare people to be a state. The Fair Hand is a rumour from ships. They have few Bound to make a book of.

Structure. The structure is town custom, slates, and vouching stretched thin. Any Kind may take up the work. The Intake’s problem is acute here and unofficial: catching a raw Struck, a shed if you’re lucky, silence if it works.

Goals. Stay whole with what is in the pot. Keep green a matter of the pot. Trade with Netstrand when a ship comes, and keep the town’s own slate.

Its injustice. No net. Vaethorn’s cruelty is guilt; Ornled’s is absence. A person who cannot barter their Tithe slides with no prayer and no guild. The loneliest unvouched in the world are next door, where no one is waiting with them.

Vaelhesk: the Far Yield

(VAYL-hesk · vael in the older sense, “what the land yields” + hesk- “far/outer edge” = “the far yield.”) Vael kept because the Old Ways claim the Motherfaith narrowed it to “gift.” Theology mid as Motherfaith gift-devotion, high as Old Ways practice. Reach low · governance low. Everyday Vaelhesk; people Vaelhesk-folk or the Yield. The fight with Vaethorn is in the first syllable: vael vs vaeth. The land is the seat. The work is host-rights.

Vaelhesk is pre-graft host-lands on Heskoren, with old greens, ancestor-doors, and first meals. The Mother’s wood is a guest. A Cutting-leave is Seat leave to cut living wood. Without the meal, that leave is a claiming. The Door-Keepers walk here as Kin-speakers. The land is the seat.

Struck and the Taken-In are read as the land’s own children. A neighbour’s week to a Motherfaith Tree can be refused on purpose: the child is already the hill’s. Vaethorn reads those children as Kept-for-want. Both families are telling the truth they have.

Site. These are Heskoren’s old greens, and the live front is only part of them. The older job of the ground is a guest-grove, and that grove is the First Bowl. The Door-Keepers walk here. The work is host-rights. The land is the seat. Vaelhesk names no city. Villages and hamlets stay on the old greens. A traveler who asks for the seat is pointed at a door. The First Bowl stays the guest-grove it already is. Harrow’s Green is the grove-town on Saelvaeth’s march.

How it formed. Animist and ancestor polities predate the wave. When grafts arrived (C.Y. 200-387, still arriving), some districts became Vaethorn. These kept the door. The state stays low because the land does not need a clerk to be owed a meal.

Structure. The structure is hearth-clusters, door-keepers, and host-right custom. Any Kind may take up the work. The Taken-In are densest here as frontier labor and as kin.

Goals. Feed the first meal. Name a Struck at the door that made them. Treat a warden with a pot as a guest or as a thief, depending on whether they sat down.

Its injustice. Host-rights as a claim that can shun. Refuse the meal, take the wood, and you are not a neighbour. A Road-hand out on this year’s road can become a trespasser for following a Seat sentence. The injustice is local and real.

Saelvaeth: the Sown Waiting

(sayl-VAYTH · sael “sowing” + vaeth “the waiting” = “sowing into the waiting.”) They still reach for the holy words because the wave is here. Theology mid-high · reach low · governance mid-low. Everyday Saelvaeth; people Saelvaeth-folk. Shares sael with Saelthael on purpose: planting on two continents. Towns a party can enter in this march are Harrow’s Green and the Three Hamlets Past the Ford. Natai is the march-town. Eolvaeth is Vaethorn’s seat, on a different road. Harrow’s Green is a grove-town in the march.

Where the wood is still arriving on Heskoren, the front runs C.Y. 200-387 and is still incomplete. Towns form where a graft has taken; between them, waiting clusters who can see a neighbour’s canopy. The march-town is Natai (NAH-tye). Dumu stands the voice at the town gate, on the road between grafts, and can be blamed for a queue the town did not write. On the Known Map, Natai sits west of Harrow’s Green, between Sanbreo and that grove-town. The hamlets stand past the ford. Harrow’s Green is a grove-town in the march, the luckiest of them, a Hand that grew up. The Three Hamlets Past the Ford can see that green. The Seat wrote them down as what the march had left over, and still does not use their folk names (Brenod · Vaelun · Ornath). The queue is a line of people waiting where you can see them. Ornsael, on Saelthael, is the same sowing, the older walk still in use on the origin continent. The city of that sowing is Larbril. The well is Ornsael.

Governance is mid-low because someone must stand for the Harrow-sentence (we Turned at Harrow’s Tree before we had our own): wardens, Road-hands passing through, a march-voice that did not exist when the first pots came. The state is large enough to be blamed, and no larger.

Site. These are Heskoren’s grafted pockets on the moving edge. The older job of the ground is a live-front Tree-town. The neighbour’s week is kept as necessity.

How it formed. The wave made it, this decade. The nexts were paying-enough, soil-enough, and chain-enough, on a list written in the Motherwood. It is a polity made of luck and a queue. Vaethorn will preach unworthiness. History does not agree.

Structure. Town-wardens, borrowed Turnings, and Road-hands who sleep here on the way to a town that was not chosen are the offices. Any Kind may take up the work. The Old Ways are neighbours, in Vaelhesk. Vaethorn heat sits on many hearths as gospel. The march they stand in is Saelvaeth.

Goals. Keep taken grafts alive. Host the neighbour’s week. Argue the next pot. Survive being the sentence the Seat already wrote.

Its injustice. The queue as a border. Harrow’s luck is politics. The hamlets can see the canopy and send children anyway. Folk-right is a temptation in a kitchen drawer. It is unofficial cutting. Spreading Trees did not make the march kind.

Gazetteer

Gazetteer types

Every Turning-place already inherits the Tree at the centre (or the neighbour’s Tree, or the wait for one); Tithe-provision as civic utility where the polity can afford it; dangerous trades on the edges; homes that accommodate becomings; the reach-edge writing the map.

Layered on top from the Years of Hands: a road that used to go further; a stone that used to be a day’s walk; an upper room that used to be a pilgrim-dorm; a house that used to be an inn. Adaptive reuse is how Ornthael looks present.

Kumbaan’s places wait on a later pass with the Open Table.

After local Trees made pilgrimage optional, a place still does one older job of the walk: devotion (the extra mile you do not need), prestige (the origin-winter you can buy), or necessity (the neighbour’s week, because your Tree is missing, young, or sick). One of those jobs per street. Devotion, prestige, and necessity can share an inn; they should not all be the town’s theme. A Held bed is a bed kept empty for a named person who has not come.

Three kinds of place the road already needed now have names. The sick-Tree town is the Mill-hold. The guest-grove is the First Bowl. The road-end is Nelath. They are written in Other Places. One kind of place is still only a kind: two claimants on one green, a tree-warden and an Old Ways door-keeper, both sure the wood is theirs to speak for. Vaelun, one of the three hamlets past the ford, leans that way. The named square of that kind is still unwritten. The First Bowl is the guest-grove. Vaelun is a kitchen past the ford.

Towns a party can walk into

These are towns a party can walk into. Each entry says where the place sits, what work it does, and who you can meet. A line in quotation marks is something a person there might say. Where an entry says the wave, it means the slow spread of living wood outward from the Motherwood. That spread is still moving. This year’s children still walk to a Tree that can speak, or a road is still in use because the walk is not over. It is the present. The Ages explains the spread at length.

Harrow’s Green

Harrow’s Green is a live-front Tree-town. It is the luckiest kind of square on this front. A scion arrived in the C.Y. 200s, spoke a generation later, and has now Turned local children for a few generations. The Harrow-sentence (we Turned at Harrow’s Tree before we had our own) is how new towns talk everywhere; here it is the decade. Everyday Harrow’s green was already a Seat sentence on a Cutting-leave. Haelin (HAY-lin) is the warden. The town is a few hundred hearths around one canopy. The march-town is Natai, west of this square, between Sanbreo and the green. This square’s water is the stream. The ford is downstream.

A grafted pocket on the Sundered Reach. Settlement clustered where the wood took. Harrow’s is old enough, for the front, to be a town. A canopy stands at the centre. The square used to be a road. Borrowed Turnings come in from the Three Hamlets Past the Ford every Eolthael.

The wave has not finished here. Harrow’s is a Hand that grew up. The three hamlets can see the green from the ford. The Seat has already written that the wood will take here and not, this year, there. Harrow’s lives inside that sentence whether it asked to or not.

Vaethorn heat sits on many hearths as gospel, not always as flag. This square lives in Saelvaeth’s orbit, the live-front march. Natai is the march-town. The Old Ways are neighbours. A first meal at a later planting is a live argument.

What this town still does, from the years when children had to walk, is necessity. Neighbouring hamlets still send their ten-year-olds here for the week. A ribbon on the stone is devotion, left by someone else. The winter you can buy is the White Note’s booking.

Site. A low rise above a stream that becomes the ford downstream. The graft took on the rise; the old pilgrim-road climbed it because that was the day’s water. Towns on Heskoren do not pick a pretty hill and then invent a Tree. The wood picked, and the hearths followed.

Water is the stream, a cistern by the warden-hearth, and the stone in the square, still the day’s drink for anyone using the road as a mile. Food is the pocket’s gardens, ranging beyond the last house, and whatever the neighbour’s week brings in Hale-month (mouths, dust, a complaint). Fuel is the same woods the Two-Bodied use as ranging. The Taken-In keep the greens around the Tree from going to seed indoors. There is no resident stiller. A travelling one is a summer’s luck.

The square is the last holy mile. Beyond the boughs the old way is a cart-track to the ford. Children are told the road goes to the Motherwood. It goes to the mill, and then to three hamlets that can see the canopy. Both are true if you keep walking.

The square. Nucleated around the canopy. Organic, not a grid. The oldest lintels face the Tree; later houses filled the wedge between the road and the stream. No walls. The boundary is the ranging and the last garden.

  • The Tree at the centre. Mature enough to Turn; young enough that grandparents remember the borrowed years. Leaf-Fall is held in the square because there is nowhere else a crowd fits. The town’s age can be read in the wood: a Hand, not a First.
  • The stone. Last mile-shrine of the road that used to go further. Climbing-scuff. Water the Road-hands still use. Ribbons some years. Haelin, town-warden, wants it moved before the next Leaf-Fall because it is in the way of the crowd. Thilim, if asked, would say it is a name. A hamlet child will climb it and ask how far it is to the Tree.
  • The upper room. Still too many beds for the present trade. Opens for the neighbour’s week; not yet a merchant-loft. A Road-hand sleeps here on the way to a town that was not chosen, sometimes with a pot. Hale-month is dust and extra mouths. Ledger-month is local.
  • The greens. Potted groves and a garden-common around the Tree, enough, not a lot with a ticket. They will not follow you home. The Taken-In here are neighbours, not a quarter.
  • The ranging. At the town edge, not a beast-yard. Two-Bodied work the woods that feed the hearth. No Kind-flag on the trees.
  • The far shed. Returned plague-and-body work, downwind, the same logic as a tannery, not an infirmary or a district.

Homes accommodate becomings: a window that opens, a room kept green, a threshold a Long-Lived waits to be asked across. Mixed Kinds. A Fox of the Wilds at the ranging is unremarkable. A Tengu on a ridge-visit is a guest.

Tithe-provision is frontier-warm and thin: the greens, the ranging, the upper room as a seasonal outlet for whoever is loud that week. No counted hall. No stiller on a licence-wall. You can be too poor to stay whole here, and the neighbour’s week will not fix that.

The square is the last mile. The road ends at the boughs. Hands-children Turn at home. The hamlets can see the canopy. They send this year’s ten-year-olds to a Tree that can speak. A Road-hand is going the other way with wood, because the walk is not over. The Harrow-sentence is this decade. It is the present.

A warden who pulls the stone down is doing theology with a hammer. Leaf-Fall needs the space. The graves do not go with the requirement, and the next necessity-column will miss the water. Haelin knows this and still wants it moved.

Offices. Town-warden (Haelin): practice-first, not Kind. Speaks the fall truly enough for a front-town. Believer-heavy the way a live Tree makes you; still has to lodge the neighbour’s week. The scion-queue is not theirs to fix, and it is the sentence they live in.

The square watches children Turn. Harrow’s witness is what a hamlet child carries home. Vouching-at-a-distance was invented for an origin-fall; here it is a walk back to the ford.

Road-hands passing through sleep in the town that was chosen, on the way to the one that was not.

A Door-Keeper at a later planting holds host-rights if a cut from Harrow’s is set in an old green. The first meal, or a claiming.

Saelvaeth’s march-voice can be blamed when the queue is ugly. The mouth that answers for the march sits at Natai.

A notice chalked on the cistern-wall, Hale-month, C.Y. 387. Haelin’s hand. The stone is not named.

The crowd will need the square. The stone will be walked around until Eolthael, and then we will see. Water stays. Names stay if they were names. Climbing is for after.

Beds above: the neighbour’s week first. A pot is not a pilgrim. A pilgrim is not a pot. I can tell. I will not always say.

If you came from the ford, you are seen here. If you must be seen at home, take a mouth with you that was here. I will not walk it for you. I have a Tree.

The hamlets will hear the notice as a slight even if it is only a crowd.

The Three Hamlets Past the Ford

The Three Hamlets Past the Ford are a waiting cluster. The Seat’s Cutting-leave already filed them as a remainder: the wood will take at Harrow’s green. It will not take this year at the three hamlets past the ford: the soil is thin there. They can see Harrow’s Green from the water. They send this year’s ten-year-olds to the neighbour’s week, or further if Harrow’s year is too full, or still the Long Mile if the neighbour is a stick in a pot. Folk hearth-names. The Seat’s paper still reads “the three hamlets past the ford.” That blindness is load-bearing. Three hearths, a few dozen houses each. You walk from kitchen to kitchen.

Three hearths past a ford on Heskoren, close enough to share a complaint and far enough that a scion for “the hamlets” would still be a fight about which green. Thin reach as weather: Kept-heavy and Struck-heavy this year, for this cohort. This year’s children still walk to a Tree that can speak. It is the present.

They have learned not to say queue. They can recite the horticulture-sentence. Folk-right cutting is a kitchen-knife in a drawer, not a rebellion with a flag. Most slips die. The ones that take are how the First Seat learns it does not own the Tree’s tissue, only the authorization.

Old Ways hearths among them may refuse the neighbour’s week on purpose. Their children are already the land’s. Motherfaith neighbours read those children as Kept-for-want. History does not agree with Vaethorn guilt.

Geographically they sit on a seam: Saelvaeth luck they can see, Vaelhesk refusal in the kitchen, Vaethorn gospel on the tongue, Ornled slate if they walk the other way.

What these hamlets still do is necessity. They make the week. They send the children.

Site. The ford is the Seat’s geography, the thing you can see Harrow’s green from, water a mile-shrine would have marked if anyone had set a stone on the waiting side. No stone. Necessity still drinks there. Children still ask how far it is.

The stream comes down from Harrow’s rise. In clear weather the canopy is a dark against the far slope. In rain it is a rumour you have to take on trust. The three greens sit on slightly different ground: a wetter shelf, an old taken plot, a thinner rise past the last hedge. A scion planted “for the hamlets” would still be a fight about which of those is soil.

Food is gardens, a shared mill-path, ranging that is just the woods. No venting-hall. No lot. Tithe-provision is the yard and the neighbour. A lapsing Condition here is a family problem before it is a guild’s.

The houses are dispersed. You walk from hearth to hearth. The cluster is a complaint with three kitchens.

Brenod (BREN-od)

Brenod is the sending hearth. Motherfaith in this kitchen is the walk to Harrow’s. They want a mouth that was there to walk back and stand for the fall, and they can recite the thin-soil sentence without using the word queue. The kitchen-knife is in this drawer more often than the others, which does not mean they have used it. It means they have looked at it.

Brenod keeps the neighbour’s week as duty. A visitor from the core will hear faith. Brenod hears a date in Eolthael.

Vaelun (VAY-lun)

Vaelun is the yield-hearth. Older vael is what the land keeps. Vaeth is the waiting. The Old Ways sit this kitchen. They may refuse the neighbour’s week on purpose. Their children are already the land’s. A first meal is the host’s claim. A later cut from Harrow’s, acting on the origin’s behalf, still wants the bowl if this ground is an old green.

Vaelun keeps host-rights. The Door-Keepers play here. Motherfaith neighbours in Brenod will call Vaelun’s Kept children passed-over. Vaelun will not correct them in the Mother’s language.

Ornath (OR-nath)

Ornath is the furthest hearth past the ford. The soil is thinnest here, still a feeling, and the horticulture is still felt rather than decided. Ornath can see Harrow’s when the weather is kind and can walk the other way, toward Ornled’s slate, if the congregation-net fails. They have not gone. The road is still a temptation that looks like sense: a ledger, the same thin Trees, and no gospel.

Ornath keeps the wait, and the other road goes toward Ornled’s slate. Some mouths there still wait. Some have stopped saying her name and have not started keeping a list either.

Which green has the sick stick is not decided. A pot in a yard, a young failure, or nothing yet: any of the three can hold it for a night. The fight about which green would receive a scion is the cluster’s civic life. Naming the hearths did not end that fight. It gave it doors.

Harrow’s has a Tree. The square down the slope is Hands. This year’s ten-year-olds still walk to that Tree. Kept-heavy and Struck-heavy is the weather this year. It is the present. A split household, shorter: one child to Harrow’s, one Kept at the ford, a sibling who will not walk the same side of the road.

There is no stone on the waiting side. There is a cup. The night before the neighbour’s week someone leaves it on the ford-rock. If it fills, they send. If it does not, the tellings do not agree, and the child still goes, or does not, according to the kitchen, and the cup does not decide. Brenod calls it weather. Vaelun calls it the land answering. Ornath has started leaving it empty on purpose, or has not.

What must exist: families who walk one child and not another (the split household, shorter); a person who will vouch a neighbour-fall all the way home, Harrow’s witness walking back to the ford (if they will not, the child is seen at Harrow’s and unvouched at breakfast); a Kin-speaker who will stand for a Struck the square will not; someone who owns a knife they will not call nameless; a Door-Keeper’s cousin in Vaelun who will ask about the bowl before they ask about a leave.

The Seat’s leave still says the three hamlets past the ford. Clerks who learn Brenod, Vaelun, Ornath have learned more than the minute wanted them to.

Said at the ford, not written. Same water.

Brenod says, “We can see it. That is not the same as having it.”

Vaelun says, “The land had them before the cart came. I will not walk a child to a neighbour to be named a Waited.”

Ornath says, “If the cup stays dry I still have to decide. The slate the other way does not ask about soil.”

None of the three will take the other two as the whole truth. The leave already named them without naming them.

The Third Hearth (and Brenthael)

The Third Hearth is a road-house that stayed a hearth. It is the third lodging out from the Motherwood on the Near Mile. Thilim (THIL-im) has kept this house since before the First Cut, as later clerks would say. Thilim is Long-Lived and inland. Seine’s bed is still made. The notes are smaller than the White Note’s. The shrine is larger. People say the third hearth. The cant brenhael is the hearth-Tree word any road-house gets. Brenthael (bren-THALE) is the neighbour-town. The house stands on Maiethorn, in Orenbren’s lodging-country. Orenbren’s capital is Rothallo. Rothallo is the Inner Close. The First Seat sits in the Motherwood.

An old Near-Mile house, three days’ walk out from Thaeloren toward the approach-country. Beds, porridge, a dry room, a green-lot, a shed. In Brenvaeth it sold the walk you could not miss twice. In Ornthael it still opens for Eolthael.

It sits in Orenbren’s lodging-country, civic beds within a week’s walk of the grove, one mouth of that lodging. The inns grew a country. This is one mouth.

What this road-house still does is devotion: it keeps the extra mile as the town’s season. The loft still sleeps people who had to come, and, rarely, people who paid to come. Thilim can tell which is which, and will not always say. The White Note is a desk on Orentel’s quay. It sells a first-year pilgrimage as a product. This house makes the bed.

Lestrand calls this “a bed-house that failed to become a bank.” Thilim asks, “Which mouths will be here at dark?”

Site. The Near Mile is a week’s door to the origin. Counted from the grove outward: first hearth a day out, second, third. Pilgrims walking in hit the neighbour-town first.

Brenthael stands a day’s walk further from the grove than the inn. It is a Hands-town that grew around a graft planted beside the road. The pilgrim-way now ends at Brenthael’s Tree. Beyond the boughs: mill, timber, the rest of Maiethorn. Children there are told the road goes to the Motherwood. It does, if they keep walking past the inn, two more hearths, and the wood. Most Hands-children do not.

The Third Hearth kept the older job: beds for people still going in. Water is a well in the yard and the stone at the lintel-road, a day’s-walk shrine that never got pulled into Brenthael’s square because the inn refused to let the town have it. Thilim uses it for water and will not join the argument about pulling it down. Brenthael’s warden wants it moved. The stone is in the way of a crowd that is not this house’s crowd.

Food is the pot, the green-lot, whatever the extra mile pays in Hale-month. The shed is still a shed.

Features.

  • The upper room. Still too many beds; not converted. Opens in Hale-month. Quiet in Ledger-month. A devotion-column, a necessity-family, and a Road-hand going the other way with a pot can sleep in it in the same week. The home-Turning is already here, and the walk is not over. A prestige household that did not book the White Note’s winter is rare and conspicuous.
  • Seine’s bed. Held. Seine of the far cape, tenth summer, not come. The mother said next year. Next year was last year, and has been, for a length of time Thilim will not convert into Cut-years. Not a shrine Thilim will admit. A shrine everyone else can see.
  • The slate. Still kept. Verso still does not match recto. Full text in the Walking Years, above.
  • The stone. At the lintel-road, beside this house. Climbing-scuff anyway. Road-hands still aim for the water. Brenthael wants it down so the extra mile does not look like a requirement on their approach. Thilim will not move a name.
  • Green-lot and shed. Still a pot and a shed. Thilim missed a green, once. That cutting stayed in the pot. The First Cut is a different knife, and a different year.
  • Brenthael’s square (the neighbour town). Tree at the centre; road ends at the boughs; Leaf-Fall as civic season; lodging-guild pressure from Orenbren. Rothallo keeps the Book. Mixed Kinds. No Kind-quarters. The town’s older job is devotion: the extra mile as a holiday. They can see the inn’s stone from the last house and resent it as a grave that is also an advertisement.

Brenthael Turns at home. The Near Mile is optional. Orenbren houses the people who still want the origin. The stone still waters a mile. Seine’s name is still on a lintel. A necessity-family from thinner soil still pays on the home-walk, because that walk is not over. It is the present. Hands can un-Hands. If Brenthael’s Tree sickened, this house would be the neighbour’s week. It has not. Thilim keeps the beds open for the people still walking.

The two fates of the same origin still stand. The White Note collected, on a Strandoren quay. The Third Hearth kept making the bed.

People. Thilim: the four Long-Lived I walked jobs, inland and tired. Blood-Read will give a true fragment. It will not tell you which job they are doing when they make the bed. Brenthael’s town-warden, unnamed, wants the stone. Practice-first. Speaks a fall in a square that used to be a road. Orenbren lodging-custom: hospitality as rent in Eolthael; Thilim charges less and is called a failure for it. This mouth is Thilim’s. Orenbren’s capital is Rothallo. The First Seat sits in the Motherwood beside this country. A pilgrim who asks Thilim where the college is will be pointed at the wood.

A chalked complaint from Brenthael’s warden-hearth, carried to the third lintel and left under the stone’s lip. C.Y. 387, after Hale-month. Thilim has not answered on paper.

The crowd will need the approach. Your stone is a mile that is no longer owed. Water we have. Names we can keep on a ribbon in the square, which is where the extra mile belongs.

You house the extra mile. We Turn at home. If you make a shrine of a bed, that is a house matter. A stone in the road is a town matter.

We are not pulling a grave. We are pulling a requirement that has ended.

The Held bed is made. If Seine comes, the stone will still be in the way of a crowd that is not hers.

Ornsael

Ornsael (orn-SAYL) is a Rain-Shadow well-town. Th is absent from the name, and the holy sound is still in the mouths. They are the Motherland’s dry east, still walking west after the core stopped. The everyday name is Ornsael. A traveller from the core who says “the west-road well” is told the town’s name. The town is a few hundred hearths, thinner than the core’s. Saelthael’s city is Larbril, where the west road meets the Well-wash, short of the pass. Ornsael is farther into the dry. Its drink is the well. Larbril’s drink, when it has one, is the wash.

East of Maiethorn’s central range, on the leeward side away from the Old Crossing: dry hills, sand-country, towns at wells. Ornsael is one of those wells. A Tree stands beside it, young for the Motherland, old enough to have Turned local children for a generation. It Speaks most years. A missed Eolthael is weather. The crisis is the drink.

Saelthael is the polity of people who still sow. Ornsael is a square of that habit. Larbril is the city. Plenty sits on the other side of the range. Pilgrimage here is necessity, and the town cannot retire it. Core pilgrims who arrive in Eolthael expecting a holiday extra mile find a necessity-column already on the west-road, and are shocked. They thought waiting was a Heskoren feeling.

Fox of the Sands hearths are densest here. That is terrain, not a flag. A town with no fox-born would be the thing worth asking about. Mixed Kinds drink the same well.

What this well-town still does is necessity. The west road is still how this year’s ten-year-olds go when a neighbour’s Turning week is cheaper than a dry summer. Devotion stays with people who already have a healthy Tree. This hill’s season is the neighbour’s week.

Site. The well is why anyone stopped. The range takes the rain; this side keeps the dust. Settlement is nucleated around water the way Harrow’s is nucleated around wood: same grammar, different scarcity. The graft was married to the well on purpose. Living earth wants a drink in sand-country as much as a child does.

The west-road is the main street. It used to be how you reached a healthier canopy, then how you reached the origin, then how you reached the core’s Hands-towns after those towns stopped coming east. It is still how this year’s ten-year-olds go, when the well is dropping or a fall is missed. Beyond the last house the road is a pale cut toward the range-pass. East of the well it peters into tracks and Taken-In plantings that look like stubbornness.

Water is the well, a few cisterns, dew-catch on the north walls. Food is terrace-gardens, goats, whatever the west-road pays when it still pays. Fuel is dung and what the range-trade carries over. The Taken-In are backbone here. Thirsty land honours green. They are not a nation.

No walls. The boundary is where the well’s courtesy ends and the dust does not.

The square.

  • The Tree at the well. Civic and sacred heart. It Speaks most years. A missed Eolthael still shuts the Given-door. Struck remains the later mercy. There is still no second-chance year. The warden Speaks when there is a fall. They will not invent a colour. The drink is this square’s crisis.
  • The well. The drink. Chalk on the lip. A share that lasts Hale-month, or does not. Theisva (THAYS-vah) keeps the marks. Lesna (LEZ-nah) keeps a planting that drinks. Bovaer (bo-VAYR) keeps the west-road names. Three mouths. The mystery is a wet knot below last year’s water-line.
  • The west-road. Still in use, because the walk is not over. Mile-markers toward the pass; one stone still in the well-square because the town grew around a day’s water. Climbing-scuff. Ribbons pointed west, which core children find backwards.
  • The well-market. Stalls in the morning shade. Fox-born densest in the crowd, not gated. A fox-market without the town being a Kind. Stock ancestries, a Tengu on a pass-errand, a Selkie who married in from the Hinge Shore and is dry and funny about it.
  • The greens. Potted, hauled, argued over. No lush lot. A cousin of the greens-keepers’ craft might winter. They will not follow you home, and home is dust if they did.
  • The ranging. Dry hills, night-work, Two-Bodied who know the difference between a sand-fox and a Fox. At the edge, not a quarter.
  • The far shed. Returned work downwind of the well, because you do not put the dead on the drink. No infirmary. A travelling stiller is a pass-story.

Homes bend to becomings: north-side cool rooms, green pots on lintels, shutters that open for a Far-Voiced hour because there is no hall to book. Thin infrastructure, high theology, low governance: Saelthael’s combination at street height.

This is Maiethorn, the Motherland, Hands-country in the core. Conservative mouths. The extra mile is supposed to be optional. Children still go west when the well drops or a neighbour’s week is cheaper. The road that used to go further is still in use, because the walk is not over. It is the present. Same sael as Saelvaeth on the live front, sowing on two continents. Both are this year.

Hands can un-Hands. A well that drops puts a Motherland square back on a neighbour’s week whether the Tree spoke or not. That is weather. The Seat will call thin soil. The town will call this year. Vaethorn-guilt imported to sand that never asked for it is the injustice: waiting read as unworthiness on the continent that invented plenty.

The well-rope is knotted at last year’s water-line. This year’s knot is wet when the water stands below it. Ornsael will not say why. Theisva moves the knot. Lesna says the planting tastes the same when it is wet. Bovaer puts sand in a west-facing shoe and does not call that a rite.

Offices. Town-warden: practice-first, not Kind. Tired of being asked if this is fox-country. May be Sands-Fox; may not. Either way the question is the slight. Speaks the fall when there is one. The well-share is the town-law. Theisva, Lesna, and Bovaer argue the marks.

The square that watches, and the west-road that watches instead. Some years the witness is local. Some years a column still walks. Vouching-at-a-distance on the Motherland is the tell that Ornthael is unfinished.

Road-hands: rare; the circuit prefers paying nexts. A pot for Ornsael is a fight with the queue.

Saelthael-folk custom: light, town-wardens, wells as civic fact. No list. Threnmaieth would like one. Plenty next door is Maiethvael’s problem.

Well-share marks, chalked on the lip, Hale-month, C.Y. 387. Theisva’s hand. The Tree is not mentioned.

Dawn-mark: below last knot. Child-jars first. Guest-jars after the planting’s one bucket, and I will hear about the planting.

West-road at dusk if the noon-mark does not rise. Bovaer has the names. I have the rope.

A man from the Reckoning asked to name the well on a roll. We named the well Ornsael, which it already was. He wrote a colour-year. The sand does not keep his hand. The knot is wet. I did not wet it.

Theisva is doing a drink. They will not file a year. Lesna will read the verso as a slight on green. Bovaer will already be on the road. Threnmaieth will file a west-road as piety or as a gap. It is a child who needs water this week.

The three seats

Eolvaeth (Vaethorn’s seat)

Eolvaeth (ay-ohl-VAYTH) is the capital of Vaethorn. It is a pilgrim-town. The name keeps th. The pulpit says Eolvaeth. Folk-speech says “Elvaeth” (el-VAYTH). The canopy speaks in some years and stays quiet in others. Vaethod (VAYTH-od) is the warden. A couple thousand souls live here in Ledger-month, and more in Hale-month. Harrow’s Green is the live-front square. The Three Hamlets Past the Ford are the kitchens at Harrow’s ford. Saelvaeth’s march-town is Natai.

A vale behind Heskoren’s Strandoren-facing coast. One of the earlier pots on this continent: a C.Y. 200s graft that took, spoke some years, and never grew into a neighbour’s certainty. The live front moved on. The waiting stayed. Congregation made a country. Vaethorn is the gospel; Eolvaeth is the square the gospel stands in.

Governance here is almost absent. What sits in the vale is a Tree, a spring, a gift-hall that is worship, and the camp-streets of people who came for a hinge and did not leave. Custom outruns written law. The town-warden is Hands-folk.

Saelvaeth’s luck is a different weather, further along the wave. Harrow’s Green is a Hand that grew up. The three hamlets can see that canopy from a ford. Eolvaeth cannot see it, and does not want to. Wanting it would be admitting the wait is a queue. The gospel says stay. Horticulture, some years, says send.

What this town still does is devotion. People walk here in order to wait. Visitors from thinner country are usually here because they have to be. The town will not call that necessity.

Site. Two tracks meet at a spring in a fold of upland. The graft was married to the water because living earth wants a drink and a child does. Settlement clustered where the wood took, and then the camps filled the fold, because a thin Tree that might speak is still the oldest Hand a long walk can reach without crossing into Saelvaeth’s orbit.

The spring is the day’s drink. Ribbons belong on other squares. Children leave a wet leaf on the lip the year the Tree does not speak. Travellers from the core will try to climb something. There is nothing to climb. The Long Mile used to pass this vale when the neighbour was a continent. The requirement ended. The wait did not.

Food is the vale’s gardens, ranging on the slopes, alms from the gift-hall pot, and whatever Hale-month mouths bring. Fuel is the same woods the Two-Bodied use as wild-leave. There is no resident stiller. A travelling blessing-surgeon is a decade’s luck. The Taken-In are the backbone, Mother’s green held in common, honoured, not a nation.

No walls. The boundary is the last camp-street and the ranging. Low governance looks like this: you can walk in, and someone will feed you, and no clerk will write you down.

The square. Nucleated around the canopy. Organic. The oldest lintels face the Tree; later houses filled the wedge between the tracks; camp-streets, cellular accretion of pilgrim-camps that learned to winter, run up the fold until they look like a town and still smell like a season.

  • The Tree at the centre. Civic and sacred heart, even when the wood is a maybe. Mature enough that grandparents remember a fall. Young enough, for a capital, that a Seat clerk would not call it a First. Some years it Turns. Some years the colour is late, or thin, or Vaethod will not call a fall that isn’t. A missed Eolthael still shuts the Given-door. Struck remains the later mercy. There is still no second-chance year.
  • The spring. The vale’s reason. Wet-leaf years. Climbing-scuff on the lip anyway, from children who have heard the stone-story. This town’s older job is the wait.
  • The gift-hall. Worship and venting in the same room (ael-hall as congregation). A Far-Voiced hour here is a hymn whether they meant it to be. No counted hall. No release-house ticket.
  • The camp-streets. Adaptive reuse at town scale. A tent-line that became a lintel. Hale-month still doubles the mouths. Ledger-month is local and guilty about the empty rooms.
  • Mother’s green. Commons around the Tree, enough, not a lot with a ticket. They will not follow you home. Home is the vale if they did.
  • The wild-leave. Ranging on the slopes, at the edge, not a beast-yard. No Kind-flag on the trees.
  • The far shed. Returned work downwind of the spring, not an infirmary or a district.

Homes accommodate becomings. Mixed Kinds. A Fox of the Wilds at the ranging is unremarkable. A Tengu on a spine-visit is a guest. A Selkie who married in from the coast is wet about the vale and kept anyway.

Tithe-provision is alms and congregation: real warmth, real want. You can be too poor to stay whole here, and the gospel will console what it cannot furnish.

Hands-children who did Turn at this Tree live here. The Long Mile is supposed to be optional. The hinge is supposed to have arrived. The Tree speaks in some years and stays quiet in others. Camp-streets still fill for Eolthael. Some years a cohort still goes toward Saelvaeth’s luck, or further if that luck is full, because the walk is not over. It is the present. The gospel says wait. The Seat’s horticulture, on a thin year, says send. Vaethod has done both. The town remembers which summers.

Devotion is the job on the street: people walk to Eolvaeth to wait together. That is how a poor land gets a capital without a crown. Orenbren’s extra mile is for people who already have a healthy canopy. The hamlets’ necessity is kitchens that can see Harrow’s. This is the wait made into a destination, and also how scarcity becomes unworthiness if you let the sermon land.

A wet leaf lies on the spring, or too many, or none. Eolvaeth will not say why. Core pilgrims ask if it is a ribbon. The year the Tree speaks, there is no leaf. The year it does not, there are too many to count, and no one files the count.

Offices. Town-warden (Vaethod): Hands-folk, practice-first, not Kind. Speaks the fall truly enough for a waiting-seat. Will not write a colour that didn’t happen, even if a visiting clerk wants the hinge tidy. The year they send a cohort toward Saelvaeth is the year the gospel and the wood disagree. They have not resigned.

The square that watches, and the camp-streets that watch instead. Some years the witness is local. Some years it has to walk home from a luckier Hand. Vouching-at-a-distance on a gospel-capital is the tell that waiting is still a road.

Road-hands passing through sleep in the town that was chosen early and stayed thin, on the way to a town that was not chosen this year.

Vaethorn-folk custom: light state, hot faith, alms. No list. Threnmaieth would like one. Ornled would not understand why anyone stays.

The unvouched Struck are the ache this seat was built to meet with faith rather than machinery. A congregation deciding whether faith or fear wins is a session, not a district.

A notice chalked on the gift-hall lintel, Eolthael, C.Y. 387. Vaethod’s hand. The Tree is not named as sick.

The hinge is this week. Beds on the camp-streets first. A mouth that came to wait is a mouth. A mouth that came to count us is a clerk, and I can tell.

If the Tree speaks we stay. If it does not I will not invent a colour. I will not send until the week is over. I have sent before. I will not pretend I have not.

The spring is not a stone. The leaf is on the lip. Do not ask me what it is for.

The gospel will hear the notice as a slight even if it is only a week.

Orentel (Lestrand’s seat)

Orentel (or-EN-tel) is the capital of Lestrand. It is a salt-city. In the name, th has worn to t. People say Orentel. Lestrand-folk will say the Salt Quay and mean the city the way they say the bed-house and mean the bank. Notes and berths meet here. The White Note House is on the third quay, north side. It is a desk in the city. Sorim (SOR-im) is the factor who charters berths and files occupancy. Tens of thousands live here. The Tree-town is the smaller half.

An inner-sea estuary on Strandoren, facing Maiethorn across the oldest water in the world. The Salt Walk used to land here. When the walk stopped being required on this coast, the berths did not empty. They changed jobs. Adaptive reuse at city scale: a Tree-town that grew a quay, then a second quay, then a leap-frog of warehouses along the tide, until the nucleated square was the smaller half of a salt-city.

Lestrand is guild self-rule carried as far as it goes. Orentel is the square that proves it: a charter-council of houses and craft-guilds speaks for the city. Citizenship-by-witness runs smoothly because reach is high. The three pillars (Long-Lived memory, Bound word, Far-Voiced feeling) are infrastructure. An unvouched person is a transaction that cannot clear.

What this quay still sells is prestige: a first-year pilgrimage, booked as a product, with empty berths in Eolthael held for households who can pay to walk a walk they do not need. People who had to come still eat in the lofts. Mataero files them as occupied beds.

Site. The estuary is why anyone stopped. Deep water, a sheltered sound, a tide that writes the working day. The graft took on the rise above the first quay (C.Y. 40s to 80s, wealthy coast, a paying next), and the oldest lintels still face that Tree. The road used to come inland from the berth toward a healthier origin-canopy. Then this canopy grew up. Then the berth became the point.

Water is the tide, cisterns on the rise, and paid wells in the warehouse-leap. Food is the river-plain, the inner-sea catch, whatever the crossing pays. Fuel is drift, peat, and what the factor-houses book. The Taken-In rent leaf-lots by the season; they are not a nation. Selkie hearths are densest on the water, a lot of shoreline, not a gate.

No walls that matter. The boundary is the last crane and the ranging-yard inland. Low governance looks like this: the council charters the guild, never the person.

Hallowquay is a lesser inner quay. A fox-born broker might still be of Hallowquay. That byname was already in the world. The seat is Orentel.

The square. Composite: nucleated Tree-town on the rise, linear quays along the tide, leap-frog warehouses where a fire or a silted berth made the next stretch cheaper than rebuilding. The interesting streets are the edges where the rise meets the salt.

  • The Tree at the centre. Still the civic heart, even when the money is downhill. Mature Hand. Leaf-Fall is a civic and commercial milestone: a family’s colour is watched for what guild it opens. The square still Turns local children for free. That free Turning is the older job the quay cannot price without noticing itself.
  • The first quay. The Salt Walk’s old landing. Empty Eolthael berths held as prestige; wool through Hale-month covering a shrine. A smell of earth out of season when a pot, not a child, is the passenger.
  • The third quay, north side. The White Note House. Ledan’s desk. Mataero’s loft in the rise behind, twelve beds, north bed not to let. Century instruments. The house is a mouth. The charter-council speaks for the city. Lestrand will only count it as success.
  • The release-house. Ael-hall as playhouse and outlet, ticketed. Superb if you can pay. The queue outside it is the have-and-have-not gap at street height.
  • The leaf-lots. Rented green, not Mother’s common. They will not follow you home, and home is a loft if they did.
  • The beast-yard. Hired ranging at the inland edge, not a wild-leave. Two-Bodied haulage is a trade dynasty if the match was priced that way.
  • The ticket-infirmaries. The Stillers on the downwind edge. Excellent. For those who can reach them. The queue is longest here too.
  • The far shed. Returned work past the last crane. Same logic as a tannery.

Homes accommodate becomings. Mixed Kinds. A Sands-Fox at a desk is unremarkable if they have warranting. The shadow economy (smuggled green, sold vouching, off-book Terms) is alleys the guilds pretend not to charter.

This canopy Turns at home. The Salt Walk is optional. The bed-house collected. Empty Eolthael berths are still shaped like a landing. Pots still take a child’s space. A First-Hand year can be booked as a winter. The berths are still in use because the walk is not over. It is the present. Hands can un-Hands. If Orentel’s Tree sickened, this quay would be the neighbour’s week. It has not. The council counts occupancy.

Prestige is the job on the street: the walk you do not need, sold. That is how a rich coast gets a capital on a charter-council. Devotion that lands here is a hymn the release-house will schedule. Necessity in the loft is Mataero’s eye. The city’s face is the winter it sells.

Salt sits on the Tree’s roots in Eolthael. Orentel will not say why. A hall-keeper will schedule around it. A book-hand will not put it in a term.

Offices. The charter-council: houses and craft-guilds. Watches the guild, never the individual. Sorim is the factor who can be asked who chartered the empty berths this Eolthael. They will answer in occupancy. Ledan is the one who writes at the White Note.

Tree-tenders: Lestrand’s word for wardens. Practice-first, not Kind. Speak the fall as a civic fact. A colour that opens a guild is still a colour.

The White Note House: Ledan writes; Mataero lets. The north bed is house custom. Two fates, two lintels.

The Book-Hands: ordinary on this quay. Signing-watch as prestige if you book it. Bound Terms stay as they were written. The Book-Hands work this quay and have no seat of their own.

Road-hands / graft-carts: rare; a pot on an Eolthael berth is cargo in the clerk’s mouth.

The Tithe-poor fade while the city steps around them. That is the injustice. Nothing is policed. Everything is priced.

A berth-slate, first quay, Eolthael through Hale-month, C.Y. 387. Sorim’s hand. The Tree is not mentioned.

Eolthael berths 1-4: held. Do not load wool. A First-Hand year is not cargo. I have explained this to the crane.

Berth 5: earth. A pot is cargo. I have explained this to the family who wanted 5 for a child. They could not hold 1-4. I did not invent the hold.

The rise still Turns for free. That is a Tree-tender matter. The quay is a hold matter. If you need both in one week you are in the wrong mouth.

White Note, third north: not a berth. Do not send me their conversions. I do not keep house-years.

Ledan will hear the slate as a neighbour who cannot convert. Mataero will hear occupancy.

The White Note House (placed, not crowned)

The White Note House is a road-house that became a desk. It is a deathless house of the trusted note. Ledan writes century-loans from a founding-summer the house calls the White Note’s count. Mataero (mah-TAY-ro) lets the loft. The north bed is house custom, kept empty, a founding-name the junior is not to copy onto a term. People say the White Note. Lestrand still says the bed-house and means the bank. Founding about C.Y. 80 (White Note year 200 = Cut-year 280, per Ledan’s conversion-problem). The quay-desk sits on Orentel’s third quay, north side; the inland loft is in the rise behind. The desk is one house on that quay.

The same fortune as the Third Hearth, later. In Brenvaeth the house was beds, porridge, and credit for a walk you could not miss twice. When the walk stopped being required in the core, the Long-Lived still remembered every debtor, and the paper outlived the pilgrimage-season. The desk sat down on Orentel’s third quay because that is where notes now move. The inland upper room is occupancy: twelve beds in the rise behind the salt, looking at a Tree that still Turns for free.

What it sells now: century instruments, trusted paper, a First-Hand year you can book (the held-over winter as product), loft-terms to wool through Hale-month, and still the old kind of note for a necessity-family: eat now, pay on the home-walk. Same paper, two jobs. The clerk who files both as occupancy does not have to see the difference.

The north bed is how a bank proves it used to be an inn. Empty is not a shrine, Mataero says. Empty is a bed they are not charging for. Ledan says the house is the same mouth. The founding-book has a name. Thilim, inland, still holds a far-cape name the White Note does not. Lestrand will only count this house as success. Orentel’s charter-council will only count it as one ticket among many.

How to get a term. A street door on the salt. No chapel-front. A counting-room that smells of ink and tide. Ledan’s stool faces the door, not the water. Mataero has the inland key.

  1. You name what you want: a century-instrument, a loft-term, a First-Hand year, the old necessity-note.
  2. Ledan asks which count the mouth wants written: house-year, Cut-year, or a third date a Lestrand court invented last generation. They can convert. They will make you say which.
  3. If the paper is a First-Hand year, the term is written here and the berth is not. Ledan will send you to Sorim for the hold and will not walk you there. Netstrand still has to charter the hull. Orenbren still has to house the sleep.

Morning at the desk is conversion. Evening is the tide-bell, then Mataero walking the rise to check the north bed. Occupied is a session. Empty is the custom.

Features. The quay-desk: third quay, north side, Orentel. Ledan’s count; conversions to Eoloren-count when a court makes them; three dates for one summer still a live query. The inland loft: in the Tree-town rise, not on the water. Twelve beds in Mataero’s slate; eight let; four open for Eolthael; north bed not to let. The founding-book: Held names as credit-tokens. Not read aloud on a term. A smell of earth out of season when a pot, not a child, is the passenger. Cargo, the clerk files. Next berth over, a First-Hand year is not cargo. Sorim will not convert that either.

No mortal court can check a Long-Lived’s memory of a ford two hundred summers back. That is why the note holds. It is also why a grandchild can owe a walk they cannot point to. Spreading Trees did not make the paper kind.

Maiethlir (Threnmaieth’s seat)

Maiethlir (my-eth-LEER) is the capital of Threnmaieth. The name keeps th. People say Maiethlir. This is where they started counting a hearth that was already holy. The First Seat sits in the Motherwood beside this country. Rithim (RITH-im) is the Speaker who names the leaf-colour at Leaf-Fall. In a small town the warden Speaks. Here the office has its own mouth. Maiethlir is a counted city, smaller than Orentel, and older. The clerks will tell you a number after every Eolthael.

A thaw-river town on Maiethorn, in the Sacred Core and not in the grove. Snowmelt comes down from Lirorn’s range; the river runs toward the inner sea. Maiethlir sat down where the water slowed enough to hold a Hand. It is an origin-town of the C.Y. 0 to 40s. It stands a day’s living-earth from the wood, on a different road than Orenbren’s Near Mile. Orenbren lodges the approach. This river counted the people who stayed.

The extra mile used to come through with the thaw. When Hands made the origin-walk optional, the lodging emptied toward Orenbren’s beds. The rooms did not stay empty. Clerks filled them. Adaptive reuse at city scale: a pilgrimage-street that learned house-names on a tablet.

Threnmaieth coils near the sacred centre and wants everyone on the Threnhael, the counted list. Maiethlir is the square that proves the coil without owning the Tree: the colour that falls is a hymn and a line. Maiethvael shares the Mother and refuses the list, same hymns, opposite paper, a day’s argument upriver or down.

What this city still does is devotion: the extra mile remains the civic season, now written under the census. People who had to come still sleep in an uncounted loft when the soil up-range is thin. People who paid book a counted winter. The crown’s roll sits on top of that older traffic. It does not replace it.

Site. The thaw-river is why anyone stopped. Ford, mill, a Hand planted beside the water because the first carrying followed living earth and a day’s drink. The Motherwood is a dark on one horizon. You can walk to the grove. Most Hands-children do not. The college is in those clearings. Maiethlir’s square is the river and the counted Tree.

Water is the river, conduits the crown maintains, cisterns in the old pilgrim-streets. Food is the Core’s plenty. Fuel is the wood’s edge, which the Seat will not let a crown cut without a leave. Proximity is pressure, not ownership. The Taken-In work the tended green the crown furnishes; they are not a nation.

A wall exists and is old. It was for floods and for a generation of argument, not for a siege the Core no longer expects. The interesting streets are inside it, where the old extra-mile meets the tablet-hall.

The square. Concentric around the canopy, then radial along the river and the old extra-mile. Ordered the way a counted city becomes ordered: not a grid drawn last week, an accretion that learned numbers.

  • The Tree at the centre. A mature Hand, not the First. Civic and sacred heart. Leaf-Fall is a joyous holy day that is also a state registration. The colour is Spoken. A clerk waits to copy it. Rithim Speaks. They do not always let the copy happen in the same breath.
  • The tablet-hall. The Threnhael’s local mouth. It stands beside the Tree. Names, colours, marriages of heritable Conditions noticed. Outsiders hear “the Counting.” Clerks say they keep the realm hale.
  • The counted hall. Ael-hall as outlet and attendance. A Far-Voiced hour furnished through the crown’s channel. To be cut off is framed as your lapse.
  • The old extra-mile. Still a street. Upper rooms that used to sleep pilgrims are clerk-dorms. A Held bed would offend the roll; the roll has not found all of them. The Watching keep a room under a chapel the wardens recut (the Grown-Over).
  • The tended green. Crown-furnished commons. They will not follow you home. Home is on file if they did.
  • The ranging-roll. Two-Bodied leave at the edge, noticed. Not a Kind-flag.
  • The crown-stiller. Excellent medicine, downwind, through the channel. The Bound are watched past their hazard, dressed as safety.
  • The far shed. Returned work past the last conduit. Filed.

Homes accommodate becomings, and the accommodation is on the tablet. Mixed Kinds. A Fox of the Snows down from Lirorn is a guest with a name. The late week here is unfiled, which is the local crime.

The clerks will tell you a number. It changes after every Eolthael.

This canopy Turns at home. The Near Mile is Orenbren’s job. The extra mile is a civic season. The Motherwood is a walk. The thaw-road still brings a necessity-family when up-range soil is thin. An uncounted loft still smells like Hale-month. The road is still in use because the walk is not over. It is the present. Hands can un-Hands. The roll would like those summers filed. Devotion is the pilgrimage the clerks moved into.

Devotion is the job on the street: people still come because the hinge is holy. The Threnhael is the layer, a tablet beside the Tree. That is how a pious core gets a capital. The grove stays in the Motherwood. The injustice is that the layer learned to withhold the green.

The thaw runs louder the week before the fall. Maiethlir will not say why. Rithim will Speak over it. A clerk may write the noise down as snowmelt. That writing is the clerk’s.

Offices. Speaker / town-warden (Rithim): Reckoned Hands pressure, practice-first, not Kind. Speaks the fall truly. The copy is a second, lesser seeing. Some summers they let it happen in the same breath. Some they do not. The crown has not replaced them. The Seat has not recalled them. That gap is load-bearing.

The tablet-clerks, Long-Lived among them, memory bent to the census rather than free to check it. Among them, someone may remember what a line was made to overwrite.

The First Seat is a college in the Motherwood, beside this country. Some Speakers cooperate with the roll (they believe they are keeping the realm hale). Some refuse. A pilgrim who asks Rithim where the college is will be pointed at the wood.

Orenbren lodging: a day’s argument along the other road. They witness who slept. The colour is Rithim’s to Speak. Threnmaieth would like the lodging-rolls.

Watchers in the oldest district: a second reading alongside the wardens. Filed as the Grown-Over.

The Kept are pitied and noted as unmarked. A household whose bloodline and upkeep are both on file cannot easily refuse the crown anything.

A Threnhael copy-slip, tablet-hall, Eolthael, C.Y. 387. The Speaker’s name is on the recto. The clerk’s hand is on the verso. The Tree is named as hale.

Recto, Rithim. Spoken: storm-grey. Witnessed in the square. The hug was not this child’s. I have said the colour. I have not said the line. The river was loud. I will not wait for it.

Verso, clerk. Copied: Two-Bodied, named to the Threnhael, household already on file. Speaker delayed the copy until after the hymn. Delay noted. River-noise noted as snowmelt. The unmarked in the same household noted. Provision continues through the counted channel.

The wood was asked whether the Speaker is a Reckoned Hand. The wood did not answer in this hall.

The delay is devotion: a pilgrimage-town that still thinks the hymn comes first. The First Seat, asked later, will say the Tree witnessed.

The twelve seats

The three famous corners are above: Threnmaieth, Lestrand, and Vaethorn, with their cities Maiethlir, Orentel, and Eolvaeth. These twelve are the other seats of power. Each one is a capital, a city, a town, or a written refusal to build a city. Harrow’s Green is a square in Saelvaeth’s orbit. The twelve seats are the ones named in this section. Six charter-towns around Raitin, the shelves of Lirorn, and the pockets between grafts stay unnamed. No capital is marked with a star on a map. The First Seat stays in the Motherwood. It is the college of tree-wardens.

Read an entry this way. The first lines say what the place is and where it sits. The work is the job the town does, and who does it. A neighbour uses a given name. A clerk in a list-land says the house-name first. The last sentences are habits. People do them and will not say why.

Seinbrun (Maiethvael’s capital)

(SAYN-brun.) Seinbrun is Maiethvael’s capital. It is a large city in the warm core of Maiethorn. It keeps no census. People call the country the Gift-Realm. Charters write Maiethvael. The city provides public greens, a hall, and medicine through its congregations and guilds. Maiethlir is the counted city. The Down-Bank is the stretch of road below Maiethlir’s Down Gate. You can spend a day among Gift-folk on that road and still be short of Seinbrun. During pilgrimage season the Motherwood is in sight. The grove stays outside the city. A town Tree stands beside the furnished hall. A child’s leaf-colour stays off any list.

The work is the hall, with the green beside it. Vuthbraen keeps the hall. Raermu keeps the medicine basket. Breillai left the hymn. The green, the hall, and the medicine went together. The hall can still set a bowl. The net stays where the hymn is. A neighbour says the given name. There is no house to put first.

The furnished hall sets an empty bowl at the end of the table, away from the pot, and clears it with the others. Seinbrun will not say why. On the day a household leaves the hymn, a neighbour stacks their green pots in the lane, still planted. They will not say why. Smaller towns around the city stay unnamed.

Rothallo (Orenbren’s capital)

(roh-THAL-oh.) Rothallo is a walled city. It is one day’s walk from the Motherwood. People still say the Inner Close, and the Close. The walls answer to those names. Rothallo is that city, and it stays inside Orenbren. Smaller towns and villages lie around it in the lodging-country. This book does not name them. The Third Hearth, the Mill-hold, and Nelath are in that country. Rothallo is the capital. Those three keep their own jobs: a hearth, a sick-Tree lodging, and the road-end.

The First Seat is in the wood beyond the wall. Rothallo is the capital. A town Tree stands inside the walls. The city does not hold a Turning at it. The Closed Heirs rank Conditioned people in the Book of Tithes, kept inside the walls. Orenbren keeps a separate witness of who slept the night. The two records are not copied onto each other. Beds for people assigned to labour are outside the gate. The Book is inside. That gate does not open to join the two.

The work is the Book. Delamem keeps it. The pages assign taxes, restrictions, and labour, on top of the Tithe a person already carries. Talnin is still the heir whose tenth year is not finished. A neighbour says a given name. A clerk says the house first. Closed on a roster is a rank kept with the house-name. The line can cast a person out and keep the house on them.

Brimaen, house Lanbru, keeps a stall inside on a licence the Book can revoke. Breolnir, house Tethnae, writes who slept. Methei, house Sithlaen, sleeps outside because the labour-line says so. The same person can be a guest on one page and labour on the other. The pages will not be copied onto each other.

The outer gate is shut for the length of a kettle’s boil, on a morning with no crowd. Rothallo will not say why. Bread for the beds outside is cut while it is still too hot to eat, and the heel is set on the wall-stone. The beds will not say why. A Closed child, the week before the tenth year, leaves one shoe by the Book’s step and walks the lane in the other. The lane will not say why.

Larbril (Saelthael’s city)

(LAR-bril.) Larbril is a medium city where the west road meets the Well-wash, short of the pass. People say the Sown Tree. Walking west, the Shelf-gate is still ahead. Walking east, the dry is in front of you.

Ornsael stays the smaller well-town, farther into the dry. Its drink is a well. Larbril’s drink, when it has one, is the wash. Dry country lies along the road between the wash and the well. The Dry Stair is another rise. People climb it so they know a dare from a Tree. The city is here because the road meets the wash.

The wash is a river in a kind Liren and a silt-line in a cruel one. A Hand stands at the meeting. It Speaks most years. A missed week is weather. Fox of the Sands hearths are dense in this country. That is terrain. The meeting is the city’s mouth.

The work is the meeting. Brormei says what the wash is this week, and whether a column goes west toward the pass. A neighbour says Brormei. There is no house to put first. Ornsael’s well-share is Ornsael’s. The pass-levy is Lirorn’s, taken at Tasain. Larbril keeps the road and the wash in one place.

When the Well-wash is a silt-line, Brormei still wets the meeting-stone from a jar. They will not say why. A cart bound west is held while the driver sets a pebble on the dry line. Larbril will not say why.

Votaer (the Hinge Shore’s port)

(VOH-tayr.) Votaer is one of the largest cities on the Motherland coast. It faces Orentel. Hulls are classified on these quays. The Hush-rate is charged here. Towns along the rest of the shore stay unnamed.

Orentel prices the crossing. Votaer sorts it. A child, a pot of living earth, a family on a neighbour’s week: the bell can sound the same, and a clerk writes which docket. The White Note stands on Orentel’s third quay. Trees on this shore are healthy. Faith is worn thinner than in Maiethvael, because a dock teaches categories.
